## dp_extract_eval

`stages/70_dp/dp_extract_eval/CHANGES.md` (70_dp, local-script)

### dp_extract_eval: changes from the original

Origin: `local-script`. The team ran `Experiment/XJF-01/extract_eval.py` on its own Windows machine, CPU only, on 2026-11-16 from 01:22:37 to 01:56:45 (+07:00). The log is in `original/extract.log`. The command was `python Experiment/XJF-01/extract_eval.py <eval_set.zip> <unzip folder> English`; the second argument is not in the log and only sets where the media are unzipped. Byte-exact copies of the files that ran are in `original/`; their sha256 values are in `original/SOURCE.json`.

| file in this folder | made from | changed? |
|---|---|---|
| `extract_eval.py` | `original/extract_eval.py` (sha256 `dad9866b…`) + script patches EE1–EE7 | yes, 7 patches |
| `flag_extract.py` | `original/flag_extract.py` = `kaggle/flag_extract.py` (sha256 `7c227c4c…`, unchanged since 27/09) | no |
| `flag_models.py` | `original/flag_models.py` = `kaggle/flag_models.py` as of 12/11 22:03, which is before the run (sha256 `9172b68e…`) + patch FM1 | yes, 1 patch |
| `stage.ipy` | new runner glue: 4 cells, each opening with `print("[cell] …")` | new |
| `models.json`, `expected_outputs.json` | new: pins and sha256 values measured on the team machine | new |

The patches are listed in `stage.json` → `script_patches` as exact `old` → `new` strings with match counts. `runner/check_stage.py` can rebuild every patched file from `original/`.

#### Script patches

| id | where | old → new | reason | effect on the numbers | type |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EE1 | `extract_eval.py` line 6 | `import io, json, sys, time, zipfile` → adds `os` | needed by EE2–EE7 | none | path |
| EE2 | line 11 | `sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "kaggle"))` → `os.environ.get("DP_CODE", …)` | the helper modules sit in the stage folder | none: same module files | path |
| EE3 | line 16 | `OUT = HERE / "feats_evalphase"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_EVAL_FEATS", …))` | the stage folder is read-only, so output goes to the stage's working folder | none | path |
| EE4 | line 17 | `KO = REPO / "kaggle" / "output"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_FEATS", …))` | the reference train arrays for the self-check come from stage `dp_feats_layout` | none | path |
| EE5 | line 36 | `zipfile.ZipFile(REPO / "Input" / "train_set.zip")` → `os.environ.get("DP_TRAIN_ZIP", …)` | the organisers' `train_set.zip` is read from `FF_DATA/00_raw`; only the 20 self-check rows are used | none | path |
| EE6 | lines 74–75 | the `except` block of the AdaFace section now ends with `raise` | the original swallowed any AdaFace error and went on. `xjf.py` would then fail later because the `face_adaface_*` arrays are missing (S7 gap 10, DESIGN WP-I) | none: on 16/11 the run took the success path (`extract.log` 01:31:53 "AdaFace check", 01:38:59 and 01:46:14 "adaface … done") | bugfix |
| EE7 | line 78 | `X.Ecapa(KO / "_ecapa_sb")` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_ECAPA_SB", …))` | the SpeechBrain savedir is prepared by stage cell 2 from the pinned revision | none: same five files, sha256 checked | path |
| FM1 | `flag_models.py`, `AdaFace.__init__`, line 123 | `snapshot_download(self.ID)` → `snapshot_download(self.ID, revision=os.environ.get("FF_ADAFACE_REVISION", "54f602a0737bd1ee4a4e7e9fd089a485f397fefd"))` | the original took whatever revision was latest. On 16/11 the team machine's Hugging Face cache held only snapshot `54f602a0…` (`refs/main` = `54f602a0…`), so that is the revision that ran | none if the hub files are unchanged; the stage checks `pretrained_model/model.pt` sha256 `e312d792…` | pin |

Every default path stays the original one: a patched script started from its original place in the team repository behaves exactly as before.

#### Stage glue (`stage.ipy`, new code)

1. **Inputs.** Locates `eval_set.zip` (dataset `ngnnguynchn/flag2027-evalphase-p`), `train_set.zip` (dataset `ngnnguynchn/flag2027-raw-v4-p`) and `dp_feats/` (stage `dp_feats_layout`), and prints the sha256 values. If a dataset is mounted unpacked, an equivalent zip with the same member paths is rebuilt in a temporary folder. Only the probed top folder is packed (`evaluation_phase/` or `train_set/`), never `dev_set/`.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* Cell 1 first checks that the Python packages of this stage can be imported (insightface, onnxruntime, speechbrain, omegaconf, huggingface_hub, torch, torchaudio, …) and otherwise stops with the list and the pinned requirement files (`docker/requirements-dp-local.txt`, `docker/requirements-kaggle-pinned.txt`). `pip_policy` stays `skip`, because the runner has no per-stage requirement subset; before, a stage run alone (`FF_ONLY`) failed late at an import.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `eval_set.zip` is taken first from the output of stage `raw_evalphase` (new input `notebooks/ff/raw_evalphase`, the zip the DP branch read), then from `DP_EVAL_ZIP` or the extracted dataset view. Unless it is byte-identical to the submitted file (`a70762a3…`), every member is checked against `stages/10_raw/raw_evalphase/expected/eval_set_members.sha256`; a differing or missing member stops the stage (`DP_ALLOW_INPUT_MISMATCH=1` to go on). Before, a zip re-built from the extracted view, which is the normal case in mode (b), was never checked.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* The temporary folder is `FF_WORK/tmp/ff_dp/<stage>` (or `DP_TMP`) and is emptied at every run. Before, it sat in the system temp folder, and rebuilt zips, unpacked media (`.done` marker of `extract_eval.py`) or model copies of an earlier run (another input, a smoke run, another `FF_DATA`) were silently reused.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `dp_feats/` is found by its structure (the one folder under the `dp_feats_layout` input that holds all 20 files of `dp_features.json`; `DP_FEATS_DIR` overrides) instead of through `dp_feats_manifest.json`, which only `dp_feats_layout` writes. A saved copy uploaded without the manifest (gap G13) now works and is checked file by file against the sha256 values.
2. **Pinned models** (`models.json`):
   - insightface `buffalo_l`: checked by the sha256 of `det_10g.onnx` and `w600k_r50.onnx`.
   - AdaFace: Hugging Face `minchul/cvlface_adaface_ir101_webface12m` at `54f602a0…`.
   - SpeechBrain: `speechbrain/spkrec-ecapa-voxceleb` at `0f99f2d0…`. The savedir is the 5 files of that snapshot, with `label_encoder.txt` saved as `label_encoder.ckpt`. SpeechBrain 1.1 reuses files that already exist in the savedir.

   A copy found under `FF_DATA/06_pretrained` is used first; otherwise the pinned version is downloaded.
3. **Run.** Runs `extract_eval.py <eval_set.zip> <tmp unzip folder> English` with the paths given through the environment. `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""` is set unless `DP_DEVICE=gpu`, because the original run was CPU only. The output is streamed line by line.
4. **Checks.** The self-check thresholds are new checks; the script itself only records the values:
   - ArcFace and AdaFace minimum cosine ≥ 0.999 on 20 train rows (16/11 run: 0.9999995);
   - ECAPA minimum cosine ≥ 0.99 (16/11 run: 0.99939).

   The sha256 values of the outputs are compared with the 16/11 run and reported; they are equal only on the same machine and libraries.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `expected_outputs.json` keyed the self-check file as `verify_english.json`, the name the team gave its copy after the later Bangla run wrote `verify.json` again. `extract_eval.py` writes `verify.json`, so that file was never compared. The key is now `verify.json` (same sha256 `9ef21fed…`, with a note); `original/verify_english.json` keeps the team's name.

#### Not changed

Feature definitions, face alignment, model calls, batching, file names and row order are those of the original scripts and modules.

**Batch padding (disclosed, kept verbatim).** The ECAPA voice embeddings of the Evaluation clips are computed in batches packed by duration (`flag_extract.run_batched` → `pack_batches`, at most 240 padded seconds and 32 clips per batch; `extract_eval.py` lines 89–90). Each batch is zero-padded to its longest clip and passed with relative lengths (`Ecapa.__call__`, `flag_extract.py` lines 165–178). The embedding of one clip can therefore vary in the last digits with the lengths of the other test clips in its batch, through convolution context at the padded end and the pooling. No label and no statistic of the test files enters, but strictly a pair's DP score is not a function of that pair alone (rule level 0). The 13.54 features were made this way (`original/extract.log`). The code is kept verbatim for fidelity; the point goes into `docs/COMPLIANCE_NOTES.md` (WP-K). Optional check after 20/11: re-extract with `max_bs=1` and report max |Δz| and the Spearman correlation of z_DP against the submission arrays.

## dp_feats_layout

`stages/70_dp/dp_feats_layout/CHANGES.md` (70_dp, new)

### dp_feats_layout: changes from the original

Origin: `new`. There is no original code to change. On the team machine, `xjf.py` and `extract_eval.py` read 20 arrays and tables directly from `<team repo>/kaggle/output/{feats_v2, feats_models, feats_eval, feats_ext_full}/`. This stage rebuilds that folder layout, `dp_feats/<sub-folder>/<file>`, from the outputs of the stages that make the files. Every file name and sub-folder is the one the scripts expect, so no script has to change its file names.

| sub-folder | files | producing stage (run in the team's work) |
|---|---|---|
| `feats_v2/` | `face_arcface_train.npy`, `voice_ecapa192_train.npy`, `voice_ecapa6144_train.npy` | `f12_eval_feats` (FLAG_12 `feats_eval/`). The arrays first came from FLAG_04; FLAG_12 re-made the same three files with identical sha256. FLAG_04 also wrote cropped variants whose crop lengths were chosen from the durations of dev files. DP never used those, and taking the FLAG_12 copies means FLAG_04 need not run at all |
| `feats_models/` | `face_adaface_train.npy`, `ext_v{1,2}_complete_face_adaface.npy` | `f10_models` (FLAG_10) |
| `feats_eval/` | `ext_v{1,2}_complete_face_arcface.npy`, `ext_v{1,2}_complete_voice_ecapa192.npy`, `ext_v{1,2}_complete_voice_ecapa6144.npy` | `f12_eval_feats` (FLAG_12, MAV-Celeb voices cut to the middle 12 s) |
| `feats_ext_full/` | `ext_v{1,2}_complete_{face,voice}_meta.csv`, `ext_v{1,2}_complete_face.npy` (VGG-Face fc7), `ext_v{1,2}_complete_voice.npy` (organisers'-type ECAPA, float32) | `r08_mav_rows` (the full FLAG_08 run of git commit `8544b3b`; its folder `feats_ext/` was renamed `feats_ext_full` on the team machine) |

`dp_features.json` lists every file with the sha256 of the bytes used for the submission. These were measured on the team machine on 18/11; the total is about 0.9 GB.

#### Behaviour

- In each producing artifact, a file is found by its exact name. If the name occurs more than once, the copy inside the producing run's own folder (`feats_eval`, `feats_models` or `feats_ext*`) is taken. A file that is still ambiguous stops the stage.
- Files are hard-linked when the artifact store allows it, and copied otherwise.
- The sha256 values go to `dp_feats/dp_feats_manifest.json` and are compared with `dp_features.json`. Re-running the producing GPU stages gives slightly different arrays, so a difference is reported and is fatal only with `DP_FEATS_STRICT=1`.
- *Fixer, 18/11.* `DP_FEATS_STRICT` now defaults to 1 (stop on a byte difference) except in `FF_MODE=train`, where the producing GPU stages are re-run; it is no longer set to 0 in `stage.json` → `env`, and an explicit value still wins. Shape and dtype of every `.npy` file are checked against `dp_features.json` in all modes. Reason: in modes (b) and (c) the arrays come from saved copies, and a copy of the wrong bundle (the empty 6485 × 0 placeholder of `flag2027-lab-data-p`, or float16 copies of `flag2027-eda-bundle-p`) was laid out with only a NOTE; `xjf.py` then trained for about 11 minutes before failing, and float16 substitutes would not fail at all.
- *Fixer, 18/11.* The downstream stages (`dp_extract_eval`, `dp_train`) no longer need `dp_feats_manifest.json`: they find the folder by its structure and check the 20 sha256 values themselves when the manifest is absent.

#### Known traps (not used here)

- `kaggle/output/feats_ext/` (150 utterances per speaker, 12 s cut, `kaggle/local_extract_ext.py`) is a different table.
- The MAV-Celeb arrays inside `flag2027-eda-bundle-p` store the organisers'-type ECAPA as float16, and `flag2027-lab-data-p` holds empty (N × 0) ECAPA-6144 arrays. Neither can replace these files.

#### To do

- **G13.** The 20 files used for 13.54 exist today only on the team machine. Upload them as `FF_DATA/02_intermediate/dp_features/dp_feats/<sub-folder>/<file>` so that this stage, and the stages that need it, can be skipped with the stored arrays (`skip_with`). No manifest is needed in the upload.
- **WP-A, data_map.** Give `notebooks/ff/dp_feats_layout` a file list (the 20 targets and sha256 values of `dp_features.json`), give `notebooks/ff/f12_eval_feats` the real hashes of the arrays the DP branch reads (`face_arcface_train.npy` `55491b54…`, `voice_ecapa192_train.npy` `b3bd0a64…`, `voice_ecapa6144_train.npy` `195490e2…`), and mark the lab-data hash `91f05874…` (an empty placeholder) as such.

## dp_score

`stages/70_dp/dp_score/CHANGES.md` (70_dp, new)

### dp_score: changes from the original

Origin: `new`. In the original work there was no separate scoring step: `xjf.py` scored the Evaluation trials in memory, right after training the final towers (original lines 166–173). Stage `dp_train` now also saves the towers (`out/dp_towers.pt`, patch XJ8), and `dp_score.py` scores from that checkpoint. Mode (b), inference with a stored checkpoint, therefore needs no re-training.

`dp_score.py` copies these definitions verbatim from `xjf.py` (sha256 `cb93cd52…`), so the arithmetic is the same:

| xjf.py lines | copied |
|---|---|
| 28–45 | `l2`, `csv_feats`, `face_cat`, `voice_cat` |
| 94 | `T` (centring and conversion to a tensor) |
| 100–105 | `Tower` |
| 131–133 | `cos` (mean cosine over the 10 seeds) |
| 166–173 | scoring loop, re-written around the loaded towers: `z = (cos − mean) / sd` with the out-of-fold constants stored in the checkpoint |

New behaviour:

- the checkpoint is loaded with `torch.load(..., weights_only=True)`, falling back to plain `torch.load` on old torch versions;
- input dimensions are checked;
- `--compare` reports, per Evaluation file, whether the ids are equal, whether z is bit-identical, max |Δz| and Spearman against another `xjf_scores.npz`.

Expected result: in the same environment as `dp_train`, the scores are bit-identical to those `xjf.py` wrote; `stage.ipy` cell 3 checks this when both files are present. With a stored checkpoint on other hardware, or with re-extracted Evaluation features (mode b), the scores differ in the last digits. The downstream check is Spearman, in `final_verify`.

#### Stage glue (`stage.ipy`, new code)

1. **Inputs.** Locates `dp_towers.pt` (kernel `notebooks/ff/dp_train`, or `DP_TOWERS`), any `xjf_scores.npz` next to it, the DP Evaluation features (`notebooks/ff/dp_extract_eval`) and `eval_set.zip` (re-zipped from an unpacked mount if needed).
2. **Run.** Runs `dp_score.py`, with `--compare` when dp_train's own scores are present; the output is streamed.
3. **Summary.** In mode train, a difference between the two scorings is flagged.

##### Fixer changes (18/11)

- **A difference from the verbatim script is fatal when the inputs are the same.** `dp_train` now records in `dp_train_check.json` the sha256 of the DP Evaluation feature files it scored (`eval_feats_sha256`). When those equal the files this stage reads, z must be bit-identical to `dp_train`'s own `out/xjf_scores.npz` (written by the verbatim `xjf.py`); otherwise the stage stops (`DP_ALLOW_INPUT_MISMATCH=1` to go on). Without `dp_train_check.json`, the same rule applies in `FF_MODE=train`. With other features (for example the stored checkpoint of the team machine and re-extracted features in mode (b)) a difference is expected and reported. Before, a difference only printed a warning, and only in `FF_MODE=train`. Reason: `final_blend` reads this stage's scores, and the check guarantees that they are the verbatim script's scores bit for bit whenever both exist (see `final_blend/CHANGES.md`).
- **Clear stop without a checkpoint.** When the `dp_train` artifact holds no `dp_towers.pt` (for instance a saved copy with only the original `xjf_scores.npz` / `consts.json`, gap G5), the stage stops with a message that names G5, `FF_FORCE=dp_train` and the upload path, instead of a bare assertion.
- **Saved output for mode (c).** `skip_with` now also lists the flat file `05_scores/xjf_scores.npz`, so that the runner can use the flat layout of DESIGN 5.3 as this stage's saved output.
- **No score statistics of the test files.** `dp_score.py` no longer prints the mean / standard deviation of z per Evaluation file, only the number of trials scored.
- *Fixer, 18/11.* `eval_set.zip` is taken first from the output of stage `raw_evalphase` (new input `notebooks/ff/raw_evalphase`, the zip the DP branch read), then from `DP_EVAL_ZIP` or the extracted dataset view. Unless it is byte-identical to the submitted file (`a70762a3…`), every member is checked against `stages/10_raw/raw_evalphase/expected/eval_set_members.sha256`; a differing or missing member stops the stage (`DP_ALLOW_INPUT_MISMATCH=1` to go on). Before, a zip re-built from the extracted view, which is the normal case in mode (b), was never checked.
- *Fixer, 18/11.* The temporary folder is `FF_WORK/tmp/ff_dp/<stage>` (or `DP_TMP`) and is emptied at every run. Before, it sat in the system temp folder, and rebuilt zips, unpacked media (`.done` marker of `extract_eval.py`) or model copies of an earlier run (another input, a smoke run, another `FF_DATA`) were silently reused.

#### To do

- `dp_towers.pt` does not exist until `dp_train` has run once (G5).
- For mode (c) through the runner, `FF_DATA/05_scores/dp/` (or the flat `FF_DATA/05_scores/xjf_scores.npz`, `skip_with`) must hold the original `xjf_scores.npz` (sha256 `686c1188…`, or `6a039faf…` for the copy that also has Bangla keys) as the stored output of this stage. WP-A owns the data_map row `notebooks/ff/dp_score` → `05_scores/dp/`. `assembly/assemble_exact.py` does not need this row.

## dp_sigma

`stages/70_dp/dp_sigma/CHANGES.md` (70_dp, new)

### dp_sigma: changes from the original

Origin: `new`. In the original work the two scale constants of the final English blend were **typed by hand** into the submission command, `XJF_SIG="5.0983,3.8376"`, from the log of the Kaggle CPU run `phantrnhuyntrn/flag-2027-nb-219-fusenet-dumpv4` (16/11). That run was deleted after an error in a copy step that came after the values were printed; its output was downloaded. No script wrote these two numbers. This stage replaces the manual step with `dp_sigma.py`.

What the constants are: SIG[protocol] is the standard deviation of the base score U = F1s′ (out of fold) + RFX (out of fold) over the English pairs of **held-out train persons** of the 5-fold person-disjoint validation SAFE-04:

- cell `v4 ng` (6 448 pairs) for the no_gender file;
- cell `v4 g` (6 448 pairs) for the gender file.

The value is the mean over the three RFX seed replicates `c0r`, `c0s`, `c0t` (Kaggle runs FLAG_213 p1, FLAG_213 p2 and FLAG_216), written with 4 decimals. No Evaluation data is involved.

The statistic is the one printed on the log line `dumpv4 <cell> <replicate>: … sd_all …` of `Experiment/FUSENET-01/run.py` (`FUSENET_MODE=dumpv4`; annotated excerpt in `evidence/fusenet_dumpv4_branch.py.txt`; the byte-exact file, sha256 `df6d739e…`, is kept in stage `t219_dumpv4` as `original/decoded/Experiment/FUSENET-01/run.py`): `g["U_<rep>"].values.astype(float).std()`, numpy with ddof 0, over the rows of one cell in table order. `dp_sigma.py` applies it to the table written by that run, `xjfdump_v4.parquet` (12 896 rows, sha256 `9f0a5af4…`).

| check | value |
|---|---|
| log, cell `v4 ng` (4 decimals) | 5.1007, 5.0980, 5.0961 → mean 5.0983 |
| log, cell `v4 g` (4 decimals) | 3.8331, 3.8481, 3.8314 → mean of the rounded values 3.8375; the full-precision mean 3.837570 gives 3.8376, the value used |
| layer-map recomputation (S7 D11/D12, S8 K3) | 5.098270 / 3.837570 → `5.0983,3.8376` |
| ddof = 1 (not used) | 5.0987 / 3.8379 |

The constants do not depend on the LoRA C1 variant: U uses only the RFX streams `t3`, `nf` and `nv`, without the ImageBind stream. In any case the three seed runs attached the original C1 dataset `onnhqunhh/flag2027-rfs-lora-p` (DESIGN N4).

#### Behaviour of the stage

- **Input.** The table is taken, in this order, from `DP_SIGMA_DUMP`; the mounted output of the FLAG_219 run (`notebooks/phantrnhuyntrn/flag-2027-nb-219-fusenet-dumpv4`, made by stage `t219_dumpv4` in mode (a), or its saved copy `FF_DATA/03_checkpoints/sigma_source` in mode (b)); then the stored copies of mode (c): flat `<FF_DATA entry>/05_scores/xjfdump_v4.parquet` (the layout of DESIGN 5.3, `assembly/inputs/README.md` and `assemble_exact.py`), `<FF_DATA entry>/03_checkpoints/sigma_source/…`, and `assembly/inputs/xjfdump_v4.parquet`. In mode (c) the runner mounts the FLAG_219 output only when a saved copy of class `checkpoint` is allowed (`FF_USE_SAVED=all`); the fallback makes the stage work with the flat layout and with `FF_USE_SAVED=collected` or `none` as well. Reading the stored table in mode (c) is the definition of that mode (DESIGN 5.3 lists it among the ten stored inputs).
- **Output.** `sigma.json["xjf_sig"]` is the exact string that `blend.py` parses, the same way as `XJF_SIG`.
- **Strictness.** `--expect 5.0983,3.8376` is always passed (`DP_SIGMA_EXPECT`). In `FF_MODE=assemble` the stage is strict by default: `dp_sigma.py --strict` stops on a mismatch, and the stage also stops when the table is not the original one (sha256 `9f0a5af4…`). In modes train / infer a mismatch is reported only, because a rebuilt SAFE-04 table may move the 4th decimal; `final_blend` keeps the pinned `XJF_SIG` of variant 13.54 there. An explicit `DP_SIGMA_STRICT=0` / `1` overrides the default. (Before: `DP_SIGMA_STRICT=0` was set in `stage.json` → `env`, so the check was never enforced through the runner; the `verify` entry used a key, `json_field`, that `runner/ffcore.py` does not read. It now uses `key` / `equals`.)
- The sha256 of the input table is compared with the original run's and reported.
- `evidence/` holds the two annotated excerpts used in this file (log lines and the code branch). They are not byte-exact copies; `original/SOURCE.json` gives the sha256 and the in-repository location of the full verbatim sources.

## dp_train

`stages/70_dp/dp_train/CHANGES.md` (70_dp, local-script)

### dp_train: changes from the original

Origin: `local-script`. The team ran `Experiment/XJF-01/xjf.py` on its own Windows machine, CPU, on 2026-11-16:

- **02:11:06 to 02:21:32 (+07:00).** Only the English Evaluation features existed. This run's `out/xjf_scores.npz` was used for the submission zip; the log is `original/xjf.log`.
- **21:52 to 22:03.** The same script, after the Bangla features had been extracted. The losses, EERs and constants are identical, and the English scores are bit-identical. Log: `original/xjf_bn.log`.

`original/` holds byte-exact copies (sha256 values in `original/SOURCE.json`): `xjf.py` (sha256 `cb93cd52…`, last modified 02:10:52), the two logs, the report and `consts.json` of the 02:11 run.

The DP branch, for Different Profile, is called XJF inside the team repository; see `docs/NAMING.md`. It trains on the organisers' FLAG train set (70 persons, 6 485 pairs) and on MAV-Celeb v1 / v2 (154 persons). The 58 MAV-Celeb voice files flagged as Bengali speech in `lid_strict.json` (key `mav_files`: 13 in v1, 45 in v2) are dropped, and the script asserts that each one was found and dropped. The training step reads no dev data and no Evaluation data; the Evaluation features are only scored at the end of the script, with constants fixed on the train set. This sentence describes the script's data flow only. It does not claim that the branch raises no compliance question: see "Compliance disclosure" below.

#### Script patches

Exact strings and match counts are in `stage.json` → `script_patches`.

| id | where in `xjf.py` | old → new | reason | effect on the numbers | type |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| XJ1 | line 11 | adds `os` to the imports | needed by XJ2–XJ7 | none | path |
| XJ2 | line 17 | `KO = REPO / "kaggle" / "output"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_FEATS", …))` | the feature arrays come from stage `dp_feats_layout`, with the same sub-folders and file names as `kaggle/output/` | none | path |
| XJ3 | line 18 | `OUT = HERE / "out"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_OUT", …))` | the stage folder is read-only | none | path |
| XJ4 | line 49 | `REPO / "Input" / "train_set.zip"` → `os.environ.get("DP_TRAIN_ZIP", …)` | the organisers' zip is read from `FF_DATA/00_raw` | none | path |
| XJ5 | line 52 | `REPO / "Input" / "meta_file_train_set.csv"` → `os.environ.get("DP_TRAIN_META", …)` | as XJ4 | none | path |
| XJ6 | line 60 | `REPO / "Experiment" / "LIDSTRICT-01" / "lid_strict.json"` → `os.environ.get("DP_LID_STRICT", …)` | the list comes from stage `l_lid_strict` (dataset `ngnnguynchn/flag2027-lid-strict-p`, version 2) | none: the stage requires sha256 `f4065711…` | path |
| XJ7 | line 85 | `FE = HERE / "feats_evalphase"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_EVAL_FEATS", …))` | the Evaluation features come from stage `dp_extract_eval` | none | path |
| XJ8 | after line 167 (final models) | adds `torch.save(...)` of `out/dp_towers.pt`, holding the state dicts of the 10 tower pairs (seeds 0, 1000, …, 9000), the input dimensions, the centring vectors `MU_F` / `MU_V` and the out-of-fold constants `CONST` | the original kept the trained towers only in memory, so no checkpoint existed (gap G5). The organisers ask for checkpoints, and stage `dp_score` scores from them | none: `train()` re-seeds torch and numpy itself; `state_dict()` and `torch.save` draw no random number and change no model or input. The line is printed rather than logged, so `report.txt` keeps its content | save |

Every default path stays the original one.

#### Stage glue (`stage.ipy`, new code)

1. **Inputs.** Locates `train_set.zip` and `meta_file_train_set.csv` (organisers), `lid_strict.json`, `dp_feats/`, the DP Evaluation features and `eval_set.zip`. A mounted-unpacked dataset is re-zipped in a temporary folder with the same member paths, packing only the probed top folder. The sha256 values are printed; the run stops if `lid_strict.json` is not the version-2 list, unless `DP_ALLOW_INPUT_MISMATCH=1`.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `eval_set.zip` is taken first from the output of stage `raw_evalphase` (new input `notebooks/ff/raw_evalphase`, the zip the DP branch read), then from `DP_EVAL_ZIP`'s path or the extracted dataset view. Unless it is byte-identical to the submitted file (`a70762a3…`), every member is checked against `stages/10_raw/raw_evalphase/expected/eval_set_members.sha256`; a differing or missing member stops the run (`DP_ALLOW_INPUT_MISMATCH=1` to go on). Before, a re-zipped view was never checked.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `dp_feats/` is found by its structure: the one folder under the `dp_feats_layout` input that holds all 20 files of `dp_feats_layout/dp_features.json` (`DP_FEATS_DIR` overrides). Before, the stage needed `dp_feats_manifest.json`, which only `dp_feats_layout` writes, so a saved copy uploaded as documented (the 20 arrays, gap G13) made the stage fail. Without the manifest the 20 files are checked by sha256.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* The temporary folder is `FF_WORK/tmp/ff_dp/dp_train` (or `DP_TMP`) and is emptied at every run. Before, it sat in the system temp folder and a rebuilt zip from an earlier run (another input, a smoke run) was silently reused.
2. **Run.** Runs `xjf.py <eval_set.zip>`; the output is streamed line by line.
3. **Comparison.** Compares the new `consts.json` with the constants of the submission run, and the arrays of `xjf_scores.npz` with the submission run through the sha256 of `ndarray.tobytes()`. The result goes to `dp_train_check.json`. Bit identity was observed only on the team machine (S8 K6); on other hardware or library versions small differences are expected.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* `dp_train_check.json` also records `eval_feats_sha256`, the sha256 of the DP Evaluation feature files this run scored. Stage `dp_score` uses it to decide whether its own scores must be bit-identical to the ones written here.
   - *Fixer, 18/11.* The summary no longer prints the mean / standard deviation of z on the Evaluation files (it printed them for information only). Score statistics of the test files are not printed by new code; see "Compliance disclosure", item 5.

#### Compliance disclosure

To be carried into `docs/COMPLIANCE_NOTES.md` (WP-K) and into the system description. The data flow of this stage follows the letter of the rules: the external data are declared, no dev or Evaluation data enter training, and the constants are fixed on train persons. The team has nevertheless identified the following risk and history, and a reader of this package must know them.

1. **The branch is weak on unseen persons.** On held-out FLAG train persons, the DP branch alone reaches only EER 25.67 (no_gender) and 34.64 (gender) (`original/xjf.log`, lines 17–18). The base system reaches about 15 / 22 on the same kind of persons. Yet adding the branch lowered the English Evaluation EERs from 13.78 / 22.12 (FACTOR_ENFUSE alone) to 10.20 / 12.98.
2. **Likely cause: recording overlap.** The most plausible explanation is that some recordings of the English Evaluation file are also in MAV-Celeb v1 / v2, on which the branch trains. A team study that simulated overlap on held-out data (team folder `Experiment/DPSTUDY-01/`) found a large gain of the fused system (+17.6 EER points on the MAV-Celeb English same-gender cell) only when the same video / recording is in training. When only the person overlaps, through other videos, the result equals that for unseen persons (the fusion then costs about 6 EER points). The team has not checked the overlap directly, because that requires matching identities across datasets, which the challenge rules forbid.
3. **Held-out test of the fusion.** A test written before running it, which applied the same fusion to held-out train persons, made the system worse (v4 gender −5.65, v4 no_gender −4.91 EER points, better in 0 of 5 folds; team folder `Experiment/XJF-01/`, README section 6).
4. **The organisers have not been asked.** A question about MAV-Celeb v1 / v2 as training data was drafted on 16/11 (`docs/BTC_QUESTION_2026-11-16.md` in the team repository) and had not been sent as of 18/11. The team regards this as a grey zone for the organisers to decide.
5. **Submission order.** `xjf_r10.zip` was submitted first: its two scale constants, 5.8 / 2.9, had been read off the score scale of the test files, which the rules do not allow. It scored 13.54 (16/11 10:31, CodaBench 1054532). Only afterwards were the constants replaced by values measured on held-out train persons (5.0983 / 3.8376, stage `dp_sigma`). `xjf_r10_trsig.zip` was then built (11:08) and submitted (12:02, CodaBench 1054704); it also scored 13.54 on average. The team made ten Evaluation submissions in all (`docs/EVAL_SUBMISSIONS.md` in the team repository); choosing among them with Evaluation scores runs against FAQ question 11 of the organisers.

#### To do

- **G5.** The tower checkpoint does not exist yet. Run this stage once on the team machine (about 12 min on CPU) and check that `dp_train_check.json` says `all_identical: true`. Then upload `out/dp_towers.pt`, `out/xjf_scores.npz` and `out/consts.json` to `FF_DATA/03_checkpoints/dp_towers/out/`.

## final_blend

`stages/80_final/final_blend/CHANGES.md` (80_final, local-script)

### final_blend: changes from the original

Origin: `local-script`. The submitted zip was written on the team's Windows machine by `Experiment/XJF-01/blend.py` at 2026-11-16 11:08:08 (+07:00); that time is stored in the zip headers. The command, recorded in the team's experiment README section 6, was:

    XJF_SIG="5.0983,3.8376" python Experiment/XJF-01/blend.py 1.0        (XJF_BASE unset)

`original/blend.py` is a byte-exact copy of the file as it is now (sha256 `ece86b2a…`). It was modified at 17:08 the same day, after the submission. The edit added the `XJF_BASE=lf` branch (lines 17–22 and the `_lf` parts of the zip names) for a later zip, `xjf_r10_trsig_lf.zip`, built on the key-fixed LoRA C1 of another system and never submitted. With `XJF_BASE` unset, the code path is the one of the 11:08 run: the layer maps recomputed all four members of the submitted zip byte for byte with this logic (S7 §0.1, S8 K1).

Recipe of the four files (the zip stores −score; lower means same person):

| file | value |
|---|---|
| `no_gender/…English_heard.txt` | (value of `posteval_FACTOR_ENFUSE_c5rl.zip`) / 5.0983 − 1.0 × z_DP |
| `gender/…English_heard.txt` | (value of `posteval_FACTOR_ENFUSE_c5rl.zip`) / 3.8376 − 1.0 × z_DP |
| `no_gender/…Bangla_unheard.txt` | value of `posteval_FACTOR_ENFUSE_c5rl.zip`, re-written with `%.8f`, so the same bytes |
| `gender/…Bangla_unheard.txt` | value of `posteval_FVBIND_ENFUSE_fvb.zip`, re-written with `%.8f`, so the same bytes |

z_DP is the DP branch score (Different Profile, internal name XJF; see `docs/NAMING.md`), standardised with out-of-fold constants of the FLAG train set. 5.0983 and 3.8376 are fixed constants measured on held-out train persons; stage `dp_sigma` recomputes them. No constant or weight of this step is computed from the test files: the formula has no part fitted at test time. That statement covers this step only; the history of the two constants and the open question about the DP branch are in "Compliance disclosure" below.

#### Script patches

Exact strings and match counts are in `stage.json` → `script_patches`.

| id | where in `blend.py` | old → new | reason | effect on the numbers | type |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BL1 | line 8 | adds `json` to the imports | needed by BL5 | none | path |
| BL2 | line 14 | `PE = REPO / "Experiment" / "POSTEVAL-01" / "zips"` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_POSTEVAL_DIR", …))` | the FACTOR_ENFUSE zip is made by stage `a_factor` | none: same file name; members checked | path |
| BL3 | line 16 | `REPO / "Experiment" / "FVBIND-01" / "zips" / …` → `Path(os.environ.get("DP_FVBIND_DIR", …)) / …` | the FVBIND_ENFUSE zip is made by stage `a_fvbind` | none | path |
| BL4 | after line 20 | adds `assert not LF` | `XJF_BASE=lf` would take the base of another, unsubmitted system; `configs/variant_1354.json` forbids it | none for 13.54: the submission was made with `XJF_BASE` unset | variant |
| BL5 | line 28 | when `XJF_SIG` is unset, read the same string from `sigma.json` (`DP_SIGMA_JSON`, stage `dp_sigma`); if neither is set, stop | the constants can come from `dp_sigma`; the original fallback 5.8 / 2.9 was read off the score scale of the test files (run 9, not allowed) and must never be used silently | none for 13.54: `XJF_SIG="5.0983,3.8376"` takes precedence exactly as before | variant |
| BL6 | line 31 | `np.load(HERE / "out" / "xjf_scores.npz")` → `os.environ.get("DP_SCORES", …)` | the DP scores come from stage `dp_score`, or the stored file in mode (c) | none | path |
| BL7 | line 44 | `(HERE / "zips").mkdir(...)` → `ZD = Path(os.environ.get("DP_ZIP_DIR", …))` | the stage folder is read-only | none | path |
| BL8 | line 45 | default weights `(0.5, 1.0, 2.0)` → `(1.0,)`, used only when no argument is given | without an argument the original wrote three weight variants; only r = 1.0 was submitted, and the team decided not to produce the others | none: the submission command passed `1.0` | variant |
| BL9 | line 47 | `HERE / "zips" / (…)` → `ZD / (…)` | output folder of BL7 | none: same zip name, `xjf_r10_trsig.zip` | path |
| BL10 | line 58 | `print("wrote", p.relative_to(REPO))` → `print("wrote", p)` | the zip is no longer under the team repo root, so `relative_to` would raise | none | path |

The formula (line 55 of the original: `o / SIG[protocol] - r * z`), the `%.8f` writing (line 57), the member order and the refusal to overwrite an existing zip (lines 48–49) are unchanged.

#### Stage glue (`stage.ipy`, new code)

1. **Inputs.**
   - Locates `posteval_FACTOR_ENFUSE_c5rl.zip` (kernel `notebooks/ff/a_factor`), `posteval_FVBIND_ENFUSE_fvb.zip` (`notebooks/ff/a_fvbind`), `xjf_scores.npz` (`notebooks/ff/dp_score`) and `sigma.json` (`notebooks/ff/dp_sigma`). Each can be overridden with `DP_FACTOR_ZIP`, `DP_FVBIND_ZIP`, `DP_SCORES_NPZ` or `DP_SIGMA_FILE`.
   - Copies the two base zips under their canonical names into a temporary folder.
   - Variant guard: `XJF_BASE` must be unset; for `FF_VARIANT=1354` (the default), `XJF_SIG` must be `5.0983,3.8376` and r must be 1.0.
   - Prints whether the base members equal the stored ones.
2. **Run.** Runs `blend.py 1.0`; the output is streamed.
3. **Members.** Writes the member sha256 values of the new zip to `final_blend_members.json`, compared with the submission.

The reference values are read from `expected.json` in this folder, an identical copy of `assembly/expected.json` produced by the same generator.

##### Fixer changes (18/11)

- **Mode (c) through the runner reads the flat layout.** Before, the DP scores were looked up only in the mounted output of `dp_score`. In `FF_MODE=assemble` that stage cannot run (kind `infer`), so the runner mounts its output only when a saved copy exists; with the ten stored files placed flat in `FF_DATA/05_scores/`, as DESIGN 5.3 and `assembly/inputs/README.md` document, the stage failed. Now, in `FF_MODE=assemble` only, when the mount has no `xjf_scores.npz`, the stage reads `<FF_DATA entry>/05_scores/xjf_scores.npz`, then `<FF_DATA entry>/05_scores/dp/xjf_scores.npz`, then `assembly/inputs/xjf_scores.npz`, which is the search order of `a_factor`, `a_fvbind` and `assemble_exact.py`. In that mode the `ids_*` / `z_*` arrays must equal the stored original (sha256 of `ndarray.tobytes()`, `expected.json` → `inputs.xjf_scores.npz.arrays_sha256`), and the constants that `dp_sigma` recomputed from the stored table must equal the pinned `XJF_SIG`; otherwise the stage stops. In modes train / infer a stored copy is never read by this stage. `final_blend_members.json` records where each input came from. Type: wiring; effect on the numbers: none.
- **Routing of the DP scores (deviation from the DESIGN 4.10 / 4.11 graph).** DESIGN draws `dp_train` → `final_blend`. The stage keeps reading `dp_score`, so that one path serves modes (a) and (b) alike: in mode (b) `dp_train` does not run and only `dp_score` has scores of the re-extracted Evaluation features. Fidelity to the script that ran is enforced in `dp_score` instead: when `dp_train` wrote its own `out/xjf_scores.npz` (verbatim `xjf.py`) from the same DP Evaluation features, which `dp_train_check.json` → `eval_feats_sha256` shows, `dp_score` stops unless its z arrays are bit-identical to those. So the values that reach `blend.py` are the verbatim script's values bit for bit whenever both exist. Before, a difference only printed a warning, and only in `FF_MODE=train`. Type: wiring; effect on the numbers: none.
- **Temporary folder.** `FF_WORK/tmp/ff_dp/final_blend` (or `DP_TMP`), emptied at every run.

#### Compliance disclosure

The full text is in `stages/70_dp/dp_train/CHANGES.md`, section "Compliance disclosure", and goes into `docs/COMPLIANCE_NOTES.md` (WP-K). In short: the DP branch alone is weak on held-out train persons (EER 25.67 / 34.64); the English Evaluation gain most likely comes from recordings of the English Evaluation file that are also in MAV-Celeb v1 / v2 (not checked by identity matching, which the rules forbid); the same fusion made the system worse on held-out train persons; the organisers have not been asked yet. Two points concern this step directly.

- **History of the constants.** The first zip of this recipe, `xjf_r10.zip`, used 5.8 / 2.9, read off the score scale of the test files (not allowed; the comment on line 26 of `blend.py` says so). It was submitted and scored 13.54 (16/11 10:31, CodaBench 1054532) before the constants were replaced by 5.0983 / 3.8376, measured on held-out train persons, and before `xjf_r10_trsig.zip` was built (11:08) and submitted (12:02, CodaBench 1054704). Patch BL5 makes sure the old constants can no longer be used silently.
- **Diagnostic printout.** `blend.py` line 43 (verbatim) prints, for each English Evaluation file, the Spearman correlation between the base score and z_DP and the mean / standard deviation of z_DP. The printout uses no label and feeds no value, so it is kept verbatim. It is disclosed because the disallowed 5.8 / 2.9 came from score statistics of the test files, the kind of number this line prints. New code in the DP stages no longer prints score statistics of the test files.

## final_verify

`stages/80_final/final_verify/CHANGES.md` (80_final, new)

### final_verify: changes from the original

Origin: `new`. In the original work the zip was checked by hand: member sha256 values, and `python Experiment/EVALPATH-U/check_eval_zip.py <zip>` for the format.

| file | origin | changed? |
|---|---|---|
| `check_eval_zip.py` | verbatim copy of `Experiment/EVALPATH-U/check_eval_zip.py` (sha256 `bde629bc…`; byte-exact copy in `original/`) | no |
| `verify_zip.py` | new: member sha256 values against `assembly/expected.json`, whole-zip sha256 (for information), and per-file Spearman / max \|difference\| against the submitted zip when it is available | new |
| `stage.ipy` | new runner glue (3 cells, each opening with `print("[cell] …")`) | new |
| `expected.json` | identical copy of `assembly/expected.json` (same generator, same bytes), so the stage still works when a code package holds only `runner/`, `stages/` and `configs/` | new |

#### Criteria

- **Format, always required.** Four files, 392 / 802 / 416 / 716 pairs, finite values, non-constant scores (`check_eval_zip.py`), plus unique pair ids (`verify_zip.py`).
- **Mode `assemble`, or `FF_VERIFY_STRICT=1`.** All four members must be byte-identical to submission 1054704 (sha256 values in `assembly/expected.json` → `target.members`).
- **Modes `train` / `infer`.** Re-run GPU stages are not bit-deterministic. Member identity and Spearman against the submitted zip are **reported**; the target in DESIGN §5.2 is ≥ 0.9999 per file. `verify_zip.py --strict-spearman` can enforce it.
- The sha256 of the whole zip is informational. `zipfile` writes the current time into each member header, so a rebuilt zip has another sha256 even when the members are identical. `assembly/assemble_exact.py` step 5 tries the submitted header fields.

#### To do

- The submitted zip is not copied into the repository while Q-D (public or private) is open. Place it at `FF_DATA/05_scores/xjf_r10_trsig.zip`, or set `FF_REFERENCE_ZIP`, to get the Spearman comparison.
- That reference zip is read straight from `FF_DATA/<entry>/05_scores/` and is not declared as an input (`stage.json` → `reads_undeclared`). Declaring it now would block the stage when the optional zip is absent: the file has no producing stage, so the runner does not treat it as a soft input. WP-A: add a data_map entry (class `score`, path `05_scores/xjf_r10_trsig.zip`, private) and declare the input once the runner supports optional inputs.
